## How the board is played — the rules of PRE-ALFA 2

Written down 2 Sep at the reset, from what two runs and the odds layer
measured. Session #6 plays these and changes nothing while it runs.

**A. The verdict line is the whole decision.** Every card marks one lane —
tip 1, or a draw-no-bet on tip 3 that out-claims it by two points — and the
guard reads that lane into one of five labels (super green → super red) from
the card's tier and its as-of confluence score. Each label carries one hit
rate frozen on two windows over 62,528 replayed picks; the card says PLAY only
when the best live quote clears that label's break-even by 6% and the tier is
not red. That is a bet. Anything else on the board is graded, not played.

**B. Flat 4% of the bankroll as it stands, and few plays.** Replayed through
two seasons at real closing prices: the whole book at 4% is a coin flip with a
near-halving drawdown; the STRONG lane compounds (€50 → €106, every year-long
start above €50). STRONG — a top-quartile confluence score in Europe — landed
81.0% at +8.87% on the 1,008 bets the bar fires on, against 72.8% and −0.67%
for the rest. Play those first.

**C. Decide at first sight, buy later if it drifts out.** The early line is
lower than the close 61% of the time on Athena's plays, but cards that clear
the bar only because the price drifted out late lost in both seasons at both
books. Thursday's verdict is the verdict; Saturday's price may be the price.

**D. The bar is a category, not the card.** Every bar built from Athena's own
number lost at real prices — the market disagrees most exactly where Athena is
most wrong. The five coarse labels stay coarse on purpose.

**E. The line that reaches the slip.** U3.0 is bought as U3.5 and U4.25 as
U4.5; everything else as printed. A match that has kicked off is not playable.
Tip 2 and double chance are never played.

The earlier rules, kept because they still hold and are the numbers the rules
above were built on:

**1. Buy at `buy≥` or do not buy.** Measured over 1,941 tips and 4,000 simulated
sequences: at threshold a book returns 1.46x, at break-even 1.01x, two percent
under 0.87x. The margin is the entire edge.

**2. Flat 4% of bankroll.** Matches quarter Kelly's growth at two-thirds the
drawdown, and never halved the bankroll in 4,000 runs. Bigger stakes on a bad
price accelerate the loss rather than rescue it.

**3. ~~High-edge tips need about 3% more price than published.~~ Now applied
automatically — `buy≥` already carries it.** Tips over +3.5% stated edge come in
2.5 points overconfident — the winner's curse of ranking by an estimate, not a
defect that can be fixed. Measured twice on separate populations at −2.5 and
−2.9, against roughly zero in every band below, so `pricing.buy_from` adds 3.2%
to those tips and nothing to the rest. **Do not add it again by hand.**

**4. In play, price the rung and not the tip.** At 0-0 half time a 2.7-goal
league gives `O0.5` 75.4% (needs 1.39) and `O1.5` 41.5% (needs 2.53). The
pre-match probability priced 90 minutes and says nothing about which of those
you are buying.

**5. The DNB confluence rule (backtested 25 Aug — refined, still
probationary).** Draw No Bet on side X only when the strands point the same
way. The 6,354-fixture replay (`dnb_confluence.py`, every pair regenerated
through the live tip code) kept the rule's core and sharpened every leg:

- **The pointed team lane is real** — the archive's 78.6% avoid-defeat
  replicated at scale: 78.1% on 3,131 confluence fixtures.
- **Leg 1 (the Over Tip 1) carries no weight of its own**: the control
  group — pointed team lane WITHOUT an over corridor — avoids defeat at
  the same 77.8%. Keep it as context, not as a strand.
- **The strand that matters is WHICH rung points.** `O1.5` direct (81.1%,
  break-even 1.32) and `U1.5` elimination (82.7%, break-even 1.27 — the
  Novorizontino shape) are the real reads. The safe-looking `O0.5` tag is
  the trap: 73.4%, needs 1.55, which no book offers for these sides.
- **Venue is a full leg.** Strong rung + X at home: 83.9% avoid defeat on
  1,231 fixtures, break-even 1.26, and it holds both windows (older 1.21,
  newer 1.31 — same direction, no flip). X away needs 1.45+ even on the
  strong rungs. Reims, Everton, Al-Shabab were all home sides.
- **Leg 4 gets a number.** "Short price confirms" is now: the offered DNB
  price must CLEAR the group's break-even — **≥1.30 for strong-rung home,
  ≥1.50 away, never on an `O0.5`-only read**. At 1.22–1.27 the strong-home
  group is roughly fair, not an edge; the 6-0 live run sits inside that
  variance. The rule finds survivors; the price decides the bet.

**6. The line-translation ladder.** When the book does not offer the tipped
line, translate DOWN in safety, never down in price. Best to worst: the
SOFTER line one notch above the tip (U4.5 over a U4.25/U3.75 tip — converts
the half-loss outcome into a full win, usually for a couple of cents); the
tip as printed at its buy≥; the HARDER half-line (U3.5 for U3.75) **only at
buy≥ + 0.07–0.10** — its win probability is identical (both win at ≤3), but
at exactly 4 goals it loses the full stake where the quarter line loses
half, and exactly-4 lands 12–14% of the time at these expectations. Below
that premium the book is keeping the insurance money.

**Minimum average odds, from settlement rather than `1 / hit-rate`:**

    Tip 1    break-even 1.211    at +5% margin  1.27
    Tip 2    break-even 1.393    at +5% margin  1.46

## The ledger of everything tried

Every feature suggestion and hypothesis put through the bar — 43 verified, 28 unfinished, 33 declined. Typed in `config/hypotheses.tsv`; this table and the app's Patches page both render from it, so they cannot disagree.

### 🟢 Verified and helping — 43

Cleared two separate time windows and is live in the engine today.

| | Date | Area | Hypothesis | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 🟢 | 09-06 | board | **Is there a cut that drops the losing plays without taking the winners with them? (the bettor, 6 Sep, after the league-badge idea failed: he had spotted MLS twice and BRA-SA once among the session's misses)** | Yes, and it is the LANE FAMILY. Sixteen structural cuts were swept over 1,220 PLAY cards, each judged only if it held in BOTH halves of a date split. The U4.5 family (rungs U4.25 and U4.5) grades 73.2% and -4.84% on 317 cards against 76.1% and +1.95% on the other 903, and the sign holds in both halves (-5.45 and -4.22). It also SUBSUMES the claim>=83 row registered two days earlier: 76% of the high-claim cards ARE U4.5 cards, and outside that family the claim effect nearly vanishes (other & claim<83 returns +2.57% on 836). The bettor's own book had been saying it independently — U3.5 15/16 at +20.4%, U4.5 13/18 at -7.1%, and every pre-kickoff loss in the session is a U4.5 or an O1.5. THE ENGINE IS NOT MISCALIBRATED, which the first reading of this got wrong and the bettor caught: across all 32,219 graded cards a higher claim does mean a higher hit rate (82-84 claims land 84.5%, 84-86 land 86.7%). The inversion exists only AFTER the price filter, which keeps 3.8% of cards. A card claiming 85% is worth 1.18, so a book offering 1.27 means the market disagrees hard; a card claiming 77% is worth 1.30, so 1.27 means it agrees. Clearing the bar therefore SELECTS for the arguments the market wins. Registered, not shipped: changing which lanes may be played is a rules change and PRE-ALFA 2 is a no-touch run |
| 🟢 | 09-02 | board | **Does striking 2-3 days out, rather than at the close, change what the bankroll replay says? (the bettor's point: the replay used closing odds, and a real strike is often days earlier)** | Yes, and not in the direction either of us assumed. football-data carries both prices — the plain columns are collected a few days before kickoff, the C columns at the close — so the same 8,506 cards were priced both ways. THE PRICE: on Athena's plays the early line is LOWER 61% of the time at best-of-market, −0.94% of price on average; the 1,256 cards that clear at the close return +1.15% at the close and −1.17% at the early price. The market drifts AWAY from Athena's side toward kickoff, which is the anti-predictive property seen from the odds screen — waiting is paid, not punished, on any given card. THE SELECTION is the opposite and larger: applying the decline bar at the early price plays 986 cards at +2.49% and compounds €50 to €101 (year-long starts median €81, 99% above €50) against €64 at the close; at one book €85 against €35. Paired, the reason is a timing signal the bar was never asked for: cards that clear both early and late return +3.40% at the close (857), cards that clear ONLY because the price drifted out late return −3.67% (399), and it holds in both seasons and at both books — −1.85/−7.71 at max, −6.90/−2.95 at Bet365. A card the market has to move toward before it clears is a card the market is leaving. The strong lane is indifferent (+10.25 early, +11.29 close on the same 195). So the operating rule is two-sided: DECIDE early — a card that does not clear at Thursday's price is not a play, whatever Saturday's says — but a card that did clear early may be BOUGHT later if its price has drifted out, because on those cards the drift is money. Registered as an instruction to the bettor, not the board: the board quotes what the API shows when rendered, and cannot yet tell a card that cleared at first sight from one that drifted in. The forward log stamps first sight, so it can grade this rule as it goes |
| 🟢 | 09-02 | data | **Is the store CURRENT for every league on the board? (a diagnostics pass over the build asked what each guard does not cover, and the rolling window's lack of a maximum AGE was the standing orange)** | No, and not for the reason the orange named. A store-wide scan of every season file found SEVEN complete seasons missing: openfootball abandoned FRA-L2, ESP-L2, GER-B2 and TUR-SL 2025-26 on 3 November 2025 and never published SUI-SL 2025-26, SWE-AL 2025 or NOR-EL 2025 at all (MLS 2025 stopped in May). The store mirrored the abandoned files faithfully, so the Swiss board — seven pending cards, three open positions — was priced on 2024-25 form plus three rounds of 2026-27; Luzern's ten-match window had six rows from April 2025. football-data.co.uk carries all eight complete, the loader already had the merge path (refresh_live, split by date so no name pairing is needed), and nothing ran it: it only ever refreshes the CURRENT season, so a season the git source dropped mid-way stays dropped. Filled from football-data — 1,921 results the store did not have, across the eight — and the 40 pending cards in those leagues re-priced. Ten changed market outright, and both Swiss unders on the book (Luzern v Vaduz, Grasshoppers v St. Gallen) now read O1.5 on the card: a season of Swiss scoring the engine had never seen. Pending clubs with half their window older than a year fell from 51 to 37. What remains is the OTHER defect, measured on the same pass: the league-controlled replay grades a window reaching 730+ days back at −1.25 mean and −2.25 weighted against the same league's fresh windows (39 leagues, 3,949 stale cards), and of the 37 left, 17 are clubs whose fresh rows sit in an ADJACENT division the store already holds — Burnley, Ipswich, Cardiff, Hull, Coventry, West Ham, Bolton, Schalke, Paderborn, St. Pauli, Girona, Metz, Heracles, Volendam, NAC Breda — with a median row age of 133-167 days next door against 490-9,268 in their own frame, where the cross-division fallback never fires because MIN_MATCHES is a COUNT and ten rows from 2024-25 satisfy it. The other 20 have nothing fresher anywhere in the store (Serie C, National, Challenge League, 3. Liga, and the dead Algerian feed). A fallback keyed on the window's age, not its size, is the next patch; the number to beat is −2.25 |
| 🟢 | 09-02 | data | **Does every club typed on the board resolve to its own history? (the bettor's standing rule: "those names need to match smoothly, no more mistakes")** | Five did not, and each had abstained with "thin history" — the engine's honest answer to a question it could not read. The check: every abstained row's two clubs, resolved in their league frame, with the frame's nearest names printed beside each miss. OH Leuven is stored as Oud-Heverlee Leuven (a first-division club with a full record, abstained on); Chongqing Tongliang Long as Chongqing Tonglianglong (one space); Atlético-MG as CA Mineiro; LAFC as Los Angeles FC; Junior Barranquilla as Atlético Junior. None is a fold case — the canonical key differs on a real token, so the day test never sees them — they are typed aliases, and all five are now in team_aliases.json against the store's own spelling. Gent v OH Leuven revived at U3.0 77.3% +7.3%. The remaining abstentions are genuine: three Algerian clubs on a feed that stopped in March 2025, and Saudi promoted sides whose second tier the store does not hold |
| 🟢 | 09-02 | board | **Does every pending card have a way to be SETTLED? (asked after Zürich v Young Boys had to be graded from a scratchpad script)** | Sixteen do not. ESPN's Swiss slug answers with the league's name and zero events on every date asked — this round, last round, last May — so the feed is dead, not lagging; Poland and Algeria have no slug at all. Seven Swiss, five Polish and four Algerian cards, three of them carrying open positions, would each have tripped the board's verify step four hours after kickoff and waited for a hand-typed mark. sweep.py now takes --set "Home v Away" 2-1: same settle() and result_market as the feed path (grade_cells is the one place a score becomes marks), refuses a fixture that has not kicked off, an ambiguous name, or a malformed score, and renders and verifies like the sweep. Found beside it: M'gladbach v Elversberg was in the "not on ESPN" list under a league ESPN covers, because the sweep's club matcher folds M'gladbach to {m, gladbach} and ESPN's Borussia Mönchengladbach to {borussia, monchengladbach} — no shared token, no prefix. Two nickname rows fix it; the same defect would have hidden any club typed by its short name against ESPN's long one. Guard slices for the eight refreshed leagues rebuilt on the fuller store: 36 pending cards moved their confluence score, none crossed a label |
| 🟢 | 09-02 | board | **Is the forward log ONE row per sighting, as its header claims?** | No: keyed by lane, and the lane a card is struck at was renamed twice on 1 Sept, so each rename re-stamped every open card — 24 fixtures under two or three lane names, Preston and Sheffield United graded as four losses instead of two, and the rule's live record read 5 plays at 20% where it is 3 at 33%. forward_settle now keeps the first sighting per fixture-date under a lane the board could actually strike; rows under a substituted rung (U3.0, U4.25) or the directional bug's output (O1.0, O0.0) are artefacts of the rename. The append-only file is untouched — the dedupe is in the reader, so the history of what was stamped stays visible. Record after: 10 settled, 3 plays at 33.3% / −57%, 7 declines at 100% / +13.6%. Too small to say anything yet, and now at least counting each card once |
| 🟢 | 09-02 | ledger | **Does the ledger CLI agree with the board?** | It did not, three ways, all the same defect — main() kept its own copy of settlement rules the module already has in bet_state and bet_prob: it crashed on every double chance (X2 into a totals pricer), dropped team lanes the card never printed (139 settled where every surface said 144), and read the cash-out multiple as a yes/no flag and then settled the position on the result AS WELL. One gate now; the CLI prints the board's 114/144 at −0.2%. Alongside: the well-formedness test had been RED since 28 Aug because it did not know the push mark, and started() pinned the board's clock at UTC+2 — right until the last Sunday of October, then an hour early for five months. Both fixed; suite 126 green |
| 🟢 | 09-01 | board | **Does Rule 6's safer neighbour apply to OVERS? (the bettor, off a Zürich v Young Boys card printing "TIP 1 O1.0 (rung O1.5)": "this needs to be normal O1.5. the U3.5 = U3.0 only applies there and with U4.25 = U4.5")** | No, and the shipped rule had it backwards. The measurement it came from is entirely UNDERS — U3.0 against U3.5 at +2.86% versus +2.32%, U4.25 against U4.5 at -4.53% versus -5.34% — and the mechanism is specific: the better buy is the NO-PUSH line, because a push returns stake at 1.00 so the edge earns nothing on that portion, and with 18% of stake pushing 18% of the money sits out. For an under that means moving UP, off the push. But bought() was written as arithmetic rather than as the two cases measured — unders up a half, overs down a half — and for an over the printed line is ALREADY the no-push one: O1.5 loses on 0 or 1 and wins on 2+, while O1.0 returns stake on a one-goal game. So the shift moved TOWARD the push, the exact direction the measurement rejected. Two consequences, both live and both visible on today's board: 159 cards printing O1.5 were quoted as O1.0, where books offer about 1.01 because it is near-certain, so no over card could ever clear the 1.27 bar and the whole side was silently unplayable; and the 50 cards printing O0.5 were mapped to `O0.0`, which is not a bet at all. bought() is now the table it should always have been, STRUCK = {U3.0: U3.5, U4.25: U4.5}, with everything else bought as printed. Nothing in the original finding changes — both substitutions and both ROI comparisons stand exactly as recorded, because both are unders. On the first re-quote an O1.5 card played that could not have before: AGF v Midtjylland at 1.31 against the 1.27 bar. THE LESSON, which is the reusable part: a result measured on two lines was shipped as a rule about a DIRECTION, and the direction was inferred from symmetry rather than tested. Symmetry is not evidence. Enumerate what was measured |
| 🟢 | 09-01 | board | **Should a fixture that has already KICKED OFF be quoted and offered as a play? (not a hypothesis anyone proposed — found while re-fetching quotes after the name fold, when three of the board's four plays turned out to be matches already in progress)** | No, and it was live. The odds feed keeps quoting a match after it starts, and those prices describe a game in progress rather than the one Athena forecast. Sheffield United v Bolton, 22 minutes played, quoted Under 4.5 at 1.83 where a pre-match line sits near 1.15 — long enough to clear the decline bar and print PLAY. Preston v Bristol City and Swansea v Watford were the same, all three kicked off at 18:45 UTC and re-quoted at 19:07. What makes it insidious is that all three were LEGITIMATE an hour earlier: the same card, the same engine, the same bar, and only the fetch time changed. The pre-match quote passes, the in-play quote passes by more, and nothing in the card says which one it is holding. Two guards, because one is not enough: odds_api.write_quotes now skips a started fixture, and webapp.verdict refuses to play one regardless of what the file says — the quote file outlives the moment it was written, so a render an hour later would go on offering a dead price. An unparseable kickoff returns False and is quoted as before, so a malformed row degrades rather than disappears. The board's playable set fell from four to one on the fix, and the one that survives is Luzern v Vaduz, tomorrow. Worth stating plainly: this had nothing to do with the name fold, it was simply the first time quotes were re-fetched mid-evening rather than in the morning, and every earlier session would have had the same exposure whenever a slate was re-quoted after kickoff |
| 🟢 | 09-01 | engine | **Should every spelling of a club be folded onto one name, store-wide and ungated? (the bettor's call after the era-split merge surfaced the same defect at a hundred times the scale: "those names need to match smoothly. no more mistakes")** | Shipped, and the honest verdict is that it is a CORRECTNESS fix rather than an accuracy one. The defect is real and was measured first: the results provider changed convention on 2024-08-10, terse trading names to full legal ones, and the store kept both, so 22.2% of every row stored since then was invisible to the spelling a hand-typed fixture resolves to — 64% in ESP-L2, 61% POR-PL, 47% ENG-PL — and Chelsea, Ajax, Porto, Roma, Juventus and Valencia each lost their entire modern record. Two layers now fold it in store.load_results: the canonical key (names.canonical, the resolver's own reduction, moved to app/data/names.py so one definition serves both instead of two copies drifting) for decoration, and 276 TYPED pairs for the case no algorithm should attempt, where one spelling abbreviates a word the other spells out — Man United against Manchester United, PSV against PSV Eindhoven, Inter against FC Internazionale Milano. 552 folds, 3 refusals. THE GUARD is a fact about football rather than a similarity threshold: a club plays at most one match a day, so two spellings sharing a date are different clubs and the fold is refused however well the names match, tested against everything already in a group so three names cannot join two clubs indirectly. All three refusals are correct (Paraguay's Club Nacional against Uruguay's, Finland's FC Inter against Inter Milan, a duplicated Hapoel row). NOW THE MEASUREMENT, which does not support an accuracy claim. Replaying 12,786 fixtures since 2024-08-10 twice, fold on and off, everything else identical: abstentions fall 496 to 317, so 179 fixtures are recovered and they grade 83.80%, right at the base rate. But on the 12,290 cards that print BOTH ways tip 1 goes 83.91% to 83.88% — nothing — and on the 263 where the fold CHANGES the rung it grades 83.65% against 82.13%, which is four cards out of 263 against a standard error of 2.32 points. Where the rung is unchanged the claim moves 0.06 points on average. So the replay says: neutral. WHY the replay cannot see the live defect, which matters more than the number: a replay looks each fixture up by its OWN stored row name, so a 2025 Chelsea match is read as `Chelsea FC` and gets the correct recent rows either way — by 2025 the long name already holds more than ROLLING_MATCHES=10. The live board is different: it types `Chelsea` by hand, which resolved to the 912-row spelling whose last match was May 2024. That is priced by the staleness study registered above (a window reaching four years back costs 1.47 points within-league) and by the board itself, where clubs read on stale rows fall from 114 to 12 and the twelve that remain are genuine — clubs that left the division. 13 pending cards re-priced, four changing market outright, all in ENG-CH. THREE FAILURES found while building it, each worse than the split it repaired: folding onto the name with most rows picks the DEAD spelling (Atlético de Madrid onto Ath Madrid, whose 456 rows all predate the change, so a board typing `Atlético Madrid` resolved to nothing — the primary is now the name the league most recently played under); typed pairs keyed by variant do not COMPOSE, so FRA-L1 stating both Brest = Stade Brestois and Stade Brestois = Stade Brestois 29 silently dropped Brest from its own club; and the fold can SHRINK what resolves, since `PSG` reached `Paris SG` through an alias whose membership guard then failed. Each is now a comment beside the code that fixes it. Green because the data defect is unambiguous and the recovered fixtures are free, NOT because the engine got more accurate — it did not, and claiming otherwise on four cards would be exactly the single-window reasoning this project has rejected six times |
| 🟢 | 09-01 | engine | **Should a club whose history is split across two names by an ERA gap be merged, when the merge file had deliberately left it split? (FC Thun and FC Vaduz: both relegated and returned, so their deep history is filed under one name and their 2026 rows under another, leaving 3 rows against a 5-match gate and two abstentions on the 2 Sep slate)** | Merged, after measuring the objection instead of accepting it. The file's own note said folding them would build a tip on six-year-old form — true, and now sized: across 68,341 replayed cards bucketed by how far back the rolling ten reaches, tip 1 grades 83.56% on a normal window (0-120 days), 82.17% at 730-1,460 days and 81.10% beyond four years. Raw that is -2.46 points, but the deep windows cluster in the cups and the Scottish lower divisions, which grade badly anyway; controlled WITHIN each league the drag is -1.47 points on an equal-league average over 35 leagues (-2.20 weighted, negative in 23 of 35). Real, one-directional and small — and the alternative is not a fresher tip but NO tip. The gate makes it temporary too: store._apply_merges only folds while the primary is under 5 rows, so the old form bridges the first weeks of a season and is then never read again. Checked first that no better source existed — SUI-CL holds two rows in total, both from a 2021 playoff, so the Swiss second tier simply is not stored. WHERE IT IS, a merge is the WRONG tool and must not be used: _apply_merges runs inside load_results, so a merged club clears min_matches and the cross-division fallback never fires — merging Coventry's 2000-01 Premier League rows onto its 2026 promotion would suppress a fallback that reads last season. Result on the board: both fixtures now price, SUI-SL's slice table was rebuilt (the league record gained 6 cards, FC Thun 84/100 became Thun 87/103), and Luzern v Vaduz came out orange at +5.63, STRONG, U4.5 at 1.41 against a 1.27 bar — the widest margin on the slate. Thun v Lausanne prices and correctly declines at O1.0 1.01. --reprice grew a --revive flag for this: an abstention is still an answer by default, but a pending abstention caused by a DATA defect since fixed was an answer to a different question |
| 🟢 | 09-01 | engine | **Should a printed rung be struck at Rule 6's SAFER NEIGHBOUR or at the rung itself? (the bettor struck U3.5 where the card printed U3.0 and noticed Unibet pays 1.45 on U3.0 against 1.29 on U3.5 — the push line pays much more and returns the stake at three goals)** | Safer neighbour, and for a reason worth keeping. First, the ROI tables were never confused: every script used lane=bought(mk) for BOTH the price and the settlement, which is correct because p_win('U3.0') equals p_win('U3.5') under the push-counts-as-hit convention while settle_fraction knows the difference (three goals pays 0.0 on U3.0, +1.0 on U3.5) — the hit-rate function priced and the money function settled. Then the comparison, both lines from the same fitted mu with the same margin and both settled on real results over 951 qualifying bets: bought line 1.35 odds, 75.0% win, ROI +0.86%; printed rung 1.49 odds, 57.0% win, 18.0% push, ROI +0.24%. The push line pays 10.5% more and returns less. By family U3.0->U3.5 gives +2.86% against +2.32% and U4.25->U4.5 gives -4.53% against -5.34%; applying the bar to the rung's own longer price is worse again at -1.53% on 1,967 bets, since the longer quote clears more often and admits weaker cards. THE MECHANISM: two forces oppose. The margin only bites the stake at risk, worth about P(push) x (1 - 1/overround) or roughly +0.95% for the push line; but a push returns stake at exactly 1.00 so the EDGE earns nothing on that portion, and with 18% pushing, 18% of the money sits out. With no edge the push line wins; with an edge you want more stake at risk, and the second force is larger by about 0.6 points. So the convention is right BECAUSE the rule has an edge — if the edge ever vanishes the better buy flips to the push line, which makes it a usable canary. Caveat per card: this is at derived prices, and Unibet's live board showed a 12.4% premium on U3.0 where the model implies 10.4%, so a book overpricing the push line by more than fair could favour it on that fixture — a line-shopping call, not a rule change |
| 🟢 | 09-01 | board | **Should measurement be restricted to the modern era? (the bettor's call: 2022-2026 is the game being played now, and a result that needs 2016 to hold is not a result about it)** | Adopted, and it costs almost nothing — which is the informative part. Recomputing every registered label rate on 2022-2026 only moves the required prices by at most 0.007: super green 89.72% -> 89.75% (bar 1.181 unchanged), green 87.37 -> 87.68 (1.213 -> 1.209), orange 83.42 -> 83.35 (1.271 -> 1.272), red 77.88 -> 77.73 (1.361 -> 1.364), super red 77.38 -> 77.78 (1.370 -> 1.363). The tier ordering the five labels depend on holds in both cuts. The registered rates therefore STAY: they survive the restriction inside noise, and re-freezing thresholds to chase a third decimal would break the property that makes the forward log gradeable, namely that a card scored today faces the same bar as one scored last month. It also corrects how the two-window split was described: 55,544 of the bank's 62,528 cards are ALREADY 2022 or later and only 11% predates it, so calling the full-bank split 'nine years against two' was true of the date range and misleading about the data — card density rose sharply as leagues were added, so the older half running 2015 to May 2024 is itself about 78% post-2022. The split has effectively been 2022-2024 against 2024-2026 throughout, which is a healthier test than the labels implied. The priced ROI tables were never affected: they run 2024-07 to 2026-05, two consecutive seasons |
| 🟢 | 09-01 | board | **Where does the decline rule's edge actually come from — the margin, or the price panel it is settled at? (asked while testing whether a looser bar could add playable lanes)** | The panel, and it is not close. Every figure in the odds work is settled at the MAXIMUM across football-data's ~10 books; re-settling the IDENTICAL rule at Bet365 alone takes +1.71% to +0.62%, and at the market average to -0.69%. Same shape at every bar: +8% gives +2.08 / +1.15 / -0.31 and +4% gives +0.66 / -0.47 / -1.51. About 1.1 points of ROI separates best-of-market from one book at the same bar — more than the margin choice and more than the per-league question tested the same day — and at the market average the rule loses money at EVERY bar. Line shopping is not a refinement, it is most of the edge. Practical consequence for a bettor holding Dutch accounts only: four books is a real panel but the +1.71% is against ten including Pinnacle, unreachable from NL, so the honest expectation sits between the columns. From one live board, Unibet showed 1.08 where Pinnacle paid 1.12 and 1.44 where GTbets paid 1.49. Recorded alongside: the margin sweep in UNITS WON rather than ROI shows a plateau from +5% to +10% (units +14 to +22, both windows positive) collapsing below +4% (-9.6 units at +0%), so loosening the bar to buy volume trades a working rule for negative-expectation bets; and letting RED cards back in adds nothing, +9.4 units against +17.3 without, with red alone at -6.60% on 119 bets |
| 🟢 | 08-30 | engine | **Is the DNB lane itself broken? (the book's 0W 4P 4L asked it)** | Deep-dived on the 15,048-fixture result-lane dump with the shipped tilt and gates replayed: DNB is the BEST-calibrated thing tip 3 does — DNB1 says 73.2, wins 77.4 of decisive (+4.2) on 3,833 prints; DNB2 +11.7; every claimed band underclaims, monotonically (+1.7 at says 65-70 up to +8.7 at 85-90); pushes 23%. The live session agrees (18W 10P 8L, 69% of decisive). The book's 0-for-11 is the ticket SHAPE, not the prediction: three unpriced own-reads, short odds under the printed buy-from, and a push rate double the expected 23% in an 11-ticket sample. Rule 7 already carries the fix. One flag for later: the 12 lane is tip 3's only OVERclaimer (says 75.3, lands 73.3 on 7,884 prints) — worth a look before any 12-heavy strategy leans on it |
| 🟢 | 08-29 | engine | **Team-lane side attribution fails on regime breaks — the bettor's hypothesis from the Yukun/Forest cards: recent staff and squad churn shows up as a form streak the engine mistakes for quality** | Measured on 8,404 replayed team-lane offers across 52 leagues (scripts/team_attrib.py): ALIGNED divergence — the last-8 scoring stub vs the long record, signed toward the rung — is monotone with overclaim: deflated +3.3, stable +1.2, mild inflation −1.7, strong (0.8+) −3.2 with both half-windows negative (−3.9/−2.5). The proposed data inputs measured differently: DEPTH of record (thin history) showed no consistent effect and shipped nothing, and league tables would have argued FOR the Yukun pick ("chasing the top"). STREAK_FROM 0.35 / STREAK_SLOPE 0.05 ships on the published number; treated region closes to +0.4/−1.0 per half; ~2.7% of offers stop printing. One-sided as always — deflated reads keep their honest underclaim. Honest limit: the debit fixes the population, not every anecdote — Yukun's own offer drew only 1.9 points because its edge came mostly from Zhejiang's base rate |
| 🟢 | 08-28 | engine | **The Playable-hit column's collapse leagues — the bettor spotted IRL-PD 83.2 falling to 68.6 on 35 lanes, CRO-1L and MEX-LMX similar** | Confirmed and diagnosed on a 29,763-tip current-engine replay (retrosim --dump). Board-wide the playable layer is honest — says 82.3, hit 82.1, gap −0.1 on 15,813 lanes — and no playable-bar height beats +1% (raising it LOWERS hit in both halves), so the bar stands. The collapse is seven specific leagues (CRO-1L, MEX-LMX, IRL-PD, MAR-BP, COL-PA, ESP-L2, ITA-SA) whose positive-edge lanes overclaim >4 points in BOTH half-windows — pooled −6.4/−7.5 — flat across edge size, while their consensus lanes underclaim +3.4: there, disagreeing with the league consensus IS the model's error. The implied proportional debit (k≈2.0/2.3) empties their playable set anyway, so the honest ship is the CONSENSUS CAP: published probability capped at the market's league-baseline chance, edge can never exceed zero, no lane there ever badges playable. Board playable hit rises 82.1 → 82.6 (82.4→82.9 / 81.8→82.3 per half). Selection untouched, as always |
| 🟢 | 08-28 | engine | **A tip claiming far more than its league's proven base rate is bragging — the bettor's hypothesis, confirmed one-sided** | Measured on 32,493 replayed tips against each league's own hit rate: within ±2 points the claims are honest (gap −0.1); past +2 every extra claimed point delivers ~−1.2 back (slope −1.27/−1.04, both windows), and the wide-high band ran 5.6 hot in BOTH halves — while wide-LOW tips deliver their claims or better, so only one side is diseased. Filtering would lift playable hit just 86.4 → 86.7 (the braggarts still hit ~84); the real leak was the CLAIM, so REL_SAYS_DEBIT ships instead: 1.1 × the excess above league+2, published number only, selection raw. Every band closes to ±1.1 in both windows |
| 🟢 | 08-27 | board | **Proof in hindsight: the losing era was volume, not the engine's ceiling** | The pre-calibration weekend — 69.9% across 73 bets taken 'just going with it', ROI −10.1% — replayed through today's engine grades 84.2% taking every Tip 1 (avg buy≥ 1.30) and 83.3% at the playable bar. On the first calibrated slate the +1% bar filters 63 lanes to 42 and hits 95.0%: selection keeps the best winners. In-sample caveat attached — these weeks are the windows the constants were tuned on, so this is a ceiling estimate; the live confirmed number (84.6% on tips published before kickoff) landing in the same neighbourhood is the corroboration |
| 🟢 | 08-27 | engine | **The weakest leagues can be lifted the way the cups were — but only under an ROI constraint** | Raised floors climb selection to safer rungs and lift hitrate in both windows everywhere — but the first pass measured only hitrate, and GRE-SL's 80.9 → 91.7 turned out to cost 66% → 6% of PLAYABLE volume (buy≥ 1.37 → 1.19): safe rungs nobody can buy edge on. Final rule: highest floor keeping ≥2/3 of playable volume. Six leagues ship 0.82, one 0.80, seven 0.78; GRE-SL and PER-L1 revert; MLS (worse at any floor) and ITA-SB (nothing to climb to) never shipped |
| 🟢 | 08-27 | engine | **Above says 90% the domestic board is too sure of itself** | Measured on 32,493 tips across 45 leagues: below 90% the gap is −0.2 (honest); at 90%+ it is −1.2 ± 0.3, holds in both windows (−1.0 / −1.5) and is STRONGER in the played population (−1.8 / −1.4). Flat inside the band, so HIGH_SAYS_DEBIT 0.012 ships on the published number — selection stays raw |
| 🟢 | 08-27 | board | **Per-league gaps at n=200 are mostly noise wearing a number** | The worst sixteen rows, re-measured at ~780 each: ROU-L1 −11.6 → −2.8, and every one of them collapsed into a band of −2.8 to +1.7 around a pooled −1.7. One row in the whole table cleared two standard errors. The table now replays up to 800 fixtures capped at two seasons |
| 🟢 | 08-27 | interface | **One update is never one match** | The sweep checks every unsettled fixture against ESPN, grades on the 90 with extra time peeled off, and refuses to leave neighbours stale — the human failure it replaces was updating one score and leaving three settled bets showing as open |
| 🟢 | 08-27 | interface | **ESPN files by the competition's local day** | A Chilean 18:00 sits on our previous date, so the sweep asked for one day and was told nothing was there; neighbouring days are now merged, which covers the Americas below us and Asia above |
| 🟢 | 08-27 | interface | **Club identity across sources** | NFD leaves ø, đ, ł and ß whole (Lillestrøm was not Lillestrom), and a translated name shares no letters with its original (Red Star Belgrade is Crvena zvezda) — folded and aliased through the nickname file |
| 🟢 | 08-27 | board | **The +1% edge bar separates rather than thins** | This run's settled Tip 1: 75.0% held (says 82.7, gap −7.7) · 86.7% at +1–3% · 100% at +3%+. The withheld band is the only one grading BELOW its own stated probability |
| 🟢 | 08-26 | interface | **The renderer must verify itself** | Every fixture on every surface, tallies matching, ledger complete, generated script parsing — a mismatch refuses to finish. Written after a syntax error blanked every page while the file still looked fine |
| 🟢 | 08-26 | interface | **A live lane should say what the score did to it** | Landed, needs N more, room for N, half gone — read from the ledger's own settlement so a push is never called a loss |
| 🟢 | 08-26 | cups | **The cup mu is blind to WHICH side is strong** | It priced from \|elo gap\|, symmetric, so a strong home side and a strong away side got the same number. B3 is the strongest term the cup lane has produced (t 4.95) and improves both windows live: 83.3 → 84.3 |
| 🟢 | 08-26 | cups | **Cup over-tips are systematically hot** | Measured 3.5 points hot in both seasons and flat across bands, so a flat debit is the right shape — OVER_SAYS_DEBIT ships, windows close at +0.0 / −0.1 |
| 🟢 | 08-26 | cups | **Hitrate-first selection beats edge-first on cups** | A probability floor of 0.82 on all six cup codes lifts ~80 → ~83 at zero volume cost |
| 🟢 | 08-26 | ledger | **A cashed-out position settles at cashout, not at the result** | The column existed and was not being read; a position cashed at stake settles 1.00x immediately |
| 🟢 | 08-25 | rules | **Rule 5, DNB confluence** | Backtested on 6,354 pointed pairs: the pointed-team lane is real at 78%, and the home+strong rung avoids defeat 83.9% — leg 4 stops being judgement and becomes a number |
| 🟢 | 08-25 | rules | **Rule 6, the line-translation ladder** | Softer line first, printed line second, harder half-line only at buy≥ +0.07–0.10 |
| 🟢 | 08-25 | engine | **Team lanes should see the opponent's defense** | DEFENSE_BLEND 0.5 — worth +7 to +11 points on team lanes |
| 🟢 | 08-25 | engine | **Top-six clashes are priced too high** | BIG_MATCH_DEBIT 0.15 goals, validated on both windows |
| 🟢 | 08-25 | data | **A league can be launched same-day** | NED-D2 taken from check data → calibrate → futurematch in one sitting, and went 4-for-4 on its first slate |
| 🟢 | 08-24 | engine | **A promoted club can be priced from its lower-division history** | Cross-division fallback at measured exchange rates (×0.754 scored, ×1.516 conceded), validated on 608 historical tips |
| 🟢 | 08-24 | engine | **The published buy≥ thresholds were all too low** | Re-derived from settlement rather than from stated probability, with the winner's-curse haircut folded into buy_from — hand rules became code |
| 🟢 | 08-24 | engine | **The team lane was split by venue and four checks missed it** | Found and fixed; the "side split" it appeared to leave behind was then measured and is not real |
| 🟢 | 08-24 | engine | **mu is over-spread** | The founding recalibration: MU_SHRINK to 0.60 with the floor moved to match, the single largest accuracy change the engine has had |
| 🟢 | 08-24 | engine | **The weakest sides are rated too low** | TEAM_RATE_FLOOR 0.95 |
| 🟢 | 08-27 | engine | **IRL-PD and MAR-BP price on a mu that carries no information** | At n≈800 IRL-PD's residual slope is −2.8 (t −3.0) and NEGATIVE IN BOTH WINDOWS — mu is anti-correlated with outcomes there; MAR-BP reads −0.8 (t −2.2). Their hitrates survive because the league mean carries them, but the stated probabilities pretend knowledge. RESOLVED 28 Aug by the consensus cap: both are in CONSENSUS_CAP_LEAGUES, so their published numbers never claim more than the league baseline and no lane there badges playable — the cull happened at the claim layer, and the tips stay on the board honestly |

Also measured while there: 301 bank days carry 112 losing days and a LONGEST LOSING RUN OF SEVEN, which is the number to hold against any five-day sample, good or bad |

ORANGE, not green, for two reasons that both cut the strong number: STRONG_SCORE = 0.71 was chosen on these same 1,008 bets, so the strong lane's ROI is in-sample by construction; and not one of the 8,517 picks sat on the line the book quoted outright (O2.5/U2.5), so every price is the book's 2.5 line carried through the engine's mu — the way every registered ROI table was built, but derived, not quoted. Instrument: scratchpad bank_sim.py; the stream is cached so a variant runs in seconds |
| 🟠 | 09-01 | engine | **When does the engine TRULY mean U3.0 rather than U3.5? (the bettor's rule: "U3.0 = U3.5 UNLESS truly U3.0")** | Never, under the current convention — and finding out why exposed something larger. winning_totals() asks the GRADER which totals win a rung, and evaluate_market reports a whole line landing on its own number as half_win so the hit-rate column can count the push, which is the project's documented convention. The consequence nobody had traced: U3.0, U3.25, U3.5 and U3.75 ALL win on totals 0-3, so they carry identical p_win, identical edge, and are one bet to the selector. The same collapse hits O1.5/O1.75 and O2.25/O2.5/O2.75. score_markets breaks ties on (-edge, -p_win), both of which are equal here, so what actually decides which of four rungs the card prints is THE ORDER OF THE LADDER TUPLE — the exact thing that function's own comment says must not settle ties ("Ties are the normal case, not an edge case, and must not be settled by the order the ladder happens to be written in"). U3.0 wins because the string sits before U3.5 in LADDER. So the U3.0 -> U3.5 substitution is not swapping one bet for another; it is giving the bet the engine chose its honest name, the one whose win condition matches the arithmetic behind the printed claim. SHIPPED: bought() now substitutes only while same_bet(rung, sub) holds, asked of the engine's own winning_totals rather than asserted, so the "unless truly U3.0" clause is enforced instead of remembered — if a future change ever makes p_win tell the two apart, the substitution stops by itself. The same test is why the over side was wrong: O1.5 wins on 2+, O1.0 on 1+, different bets, and the old arithmetic swapped one for the other. NOT FIXED, and the reason it is orange: making the engine able to mean U3.0 requires p_win to stop counting the push as a win, which splits the selection convention from the hit-rate convention and re-prices every card in the bank. That invalidates every registered baseline, every calibration and every ROI table in docs/, so it is a project, not a patch. Worth noting the prize if it is ever done: four rungs currently indistinguishable would become four real choices, and the selector would be picking among them on evidence instead of on tuple order |
| 🟠 | 09-01 | engine | **How widespread is the split-name defect the merge file patches club by club? (asked because the scan for era splits was run store-wide rather than on the two clubs that surfaced)** | Far wider than the merge file can reach, and it is a RECENCY defect wearing a naming costume. The results provider changed convention on 2024-08-10 — short trading names became full legal ones — and the store kept both, so most clubs in the major leagues now hold two or three spellings: Chelsea 912 rows to 2024-05-19 alongside Chelsea FC 76 rows covering the last two seasons; Man United under Man United, Manchester United AND Manchester United FC. Grouping every name in every league by a normalised key and asking the REAL resolver which spelling a hand-typed fixture lands on, 8,329 of the 37,556 rows stored since Aug 2024 — 22.2% — are invisible to the name that wins. ESP-L2 64.2%, POR-PL 60.7%, ENG-PL 47.4%, ESP-LL 44.7%, ENG-CH 31.3%. Ajax, Porto, Roma, Juventus, Valencia, Getafe and Chelsea each lose 70-77 recent rows, i.e. their entire modern record. This is NOT what team_merges fixes: that gate fires only when the primary is under 5 rows, and Chelsea has 912, so the merge mechanism is structurally unable to see it. It is also not currently costing the board much — 1 of 37 pending cards reads stale rows (Yokohama FM, resolved onto Yokohama FC, the exact failure the aliases file header warns about) — because ingest_board keeps feeding SHORT names back in, which keeps the short spelling roughly current for any club that has already appeared. Chelsea has not, so the first Chelsea card will price on spring-2024 form. NOT FIXED, deliberately: unconditional canonicalisation rewrites club history in 27 leagues and therefore every number measured on them, which is a call for the bettor rather than a tidy-up. The staleness measurement above prices the damage at roughly 1.5 points of hit rate per affected card |
| 🟠 | 09-01 | board | **Is the SYNTHETIC draw-no-bet price behind the +6.05% actually reachable? (it was built from the best home and best away quote across all books, assuming both takeable at once and carrying no DNB-market margin — if a real quote is 6% worse the edge is gone)** | PASSES, at about +3.7%. Needs no history and no settlement, only today's board: fetch draw_no_bet and h2h for the same upcoming fixture in one request and compare. 60 credits, 24 fixtures, 48 comparable prices. Every single real quote is worse than the synthetic — no book gives the arbitrage away — but the shading is NOT flat with price: books shade hardest on longshots (2.60+ band, mean -6.07%, worst -16.30%) and barely at all on short favourites (1.00-1.25 band, mean -1.81%, worst -2.38%). The gated DNBs average 1.160, at the tight end. In the band actually bet (1.00-1.45) the mean gap is -2.50% and the median -2.34%, which takes the measured +6.04% to +3.66% / +3.81% rather than killing it. Break-even sits at P=1.086, a gap of -6.3%, and NONE of the 15 short-price quotes were worse than that. Two constraints the probe also found: draw-no-bet is quoted by only 1 to 4 books per fixture against 7 to 13 for the match odds, which caps stake rather than price; and NED-ED and POR-PL carry no DNB market at all, 6 of 30 fixtures probed, so a gated DNB there cannot be played as one. Residual uncertainty stated rather than hidden: the backtest's synthetic came from football-data MAX CLOSING while this probe's came from odds-api EU books pre-match, so if the former runs higher the true haircut is larger than -2.50% and this estimate is optimistic by that difference. The headroom absorbs a fair amount of it, but the number to trust is the forward log — n is 48 prices on one day against a 212-bet base result |
| 🟠 | 09-01 | board | **Can the guard decline on the PRICE rather than on the card? (the bettor's question: pick = tip X, but odds not worthy, so DECLINE — each label carries a measured hit rate, so it implies a break-even of 1/hit)** | The first positive return in the whole odds line of work, and it is mostly one lane. Bars learned on the OLDER half and applied to the newer, which is cleanly out-of-sample by accident of coverage — football-data holds two seasons against the bank's four, so all 8,121 priced cards sit in the newer half and none touched the bars judging them. The gradient runs monotonically from -1.67% playing everything to -0.21% at break-even+4%, +0.83% at +6%, +1.49% at +8% and +1.59% at +12%, crossing zero around +5% — while the HIT RATE falls the whole way, 81.8% to 69.8%. That is the point: every earlier rule selected for hit rate and watched the quote shorten by exactly as much, whereas this one selects for cards the market has not shortened ENOUGH, and it is the first rule here hunting a book error rather than an engine strength. No single cell is significant (+/-1.77 on +0.83); the gradient across seven thresholds is the evidence. Two controls survived. The label beats a plain price bar at matched selectivity by 1.2-1.3 points at the tighter bars (+0.83 vs -0.41 at +6%, +1.49 vs +0.18 at +8%), and nothing at +4%. And it is NOT a fitted-mu artifact: selecting on a derived price while settling at the same derived price would bias returns upward where extrapolation is worst, but the effect is +1.48% in the moderate band (0.6-1.1 from the 2.5 line) and -4.53% in the heavy one (1.6+), the opposite signature. HOWEVER the ladder alone only reaches break-even: -1.88% playing everything to +0.03% at +6% and +0.69% at +8%, inside its error bars. The DNB lane carries it — 212 bets at +6.05% +/-1.98 with NO price bar, rising to +15.92% on the 57 kept at +6%, on prices that are exact rather than fitted (arithmetic from the book's 1X2, no Poisson step) and splitting +6.14% older against +5.96% newer. The market implies 86.2% of decisive there and Athena wins 92.0%, which is the same underclaim found on 30 Aug now showing up as money. 65 of the 90 kept super-green cards are these DNBs. Orange for one reason: the DNB price is SYNTHESISED from the best home and best away quote across all books, which assumes both are takeable at once and carries no DNB-market margin of its own, so a real single-book draw-no-bet quote will be worse. Settling that against real quoted draw_no_bet prices now outranks the team-total probe — tip 2's signal is larger in hit-rate terms but has never shown a positive return at any price, and this just did |
| 🟠 | 09-01 | board | **Can a card be scored by running it back through the board's OWN searches — the league's record on that lane, then filtered to each club, to the tip's side, and to club-and-side — counting each slice above or below the league baseline as a flag for or against, then pivoting or declining when the sum is bad? (the bettor's rule, worked by hand on Aston Villa v Arsenal 31 Aug, which printed O1.5 82.0% and finished 0-1)** | Real, and almost entirely a restatement of the claim the card already prints. scripts/confluence.py builds every slice STRICTLY AS-OF in one forward pass — the search bar looks backwards over completed matches, so a slice it shows today contains the fixture you would have bet on, and scored that way the rule cannot fail. Over 25,494 graded cards in 57 leagues the continuous score is monotone across all ten deciles (80.62% at the bottom to 87.39% at the top) and keeping the top 40% grades 86.06% against an 83.65% base, older 86.04 newer 86.07. The hand-worked example reproduces exactly: score -2, matching the bettor's own count, with every slice within a few points of their board reading. But the ablation kills the novel part: the claim-vs-baseline check ALONE lifts +3.81 while all six searches together lift only +1.98, clubs-only lifts +0.18, and adding the club slices HALVES the claim signal. Head-to-head at matched selectivity the printed claim beats the score at every cut (+3.79 vs +2.40 keeping the top 40%), and where they disagree the claim is right — score-keeps-claim-drops grades 83.20% (below base) against claim-keeps-score-drops at 86.17%. Held fixed inside claim deciles the residual is +1.34 points, +2.14 older and +0.90 newer. Three further no's: inside the guard's RED tier the score INVERTS (79.4% at <=-2 against 76.2% at >=+2), so it can demote but never promote; no pivot beats standing the condemned pick (stand 82.22%, tip 3 78.17% even where one exists, tip 2 68.29%), so only NONE is better; and priced on 5,981 rungs at real closing odds the ROI goes -1.86% to -1.51% with the whole gain in the older window (-1.25 to -0.15) while the newer one worsens (-2.47 to -2.84). Depth does NOT help — beyond ten cards of club history the effect is flat at +2.4 to +3.3 and the sharpest separation is in the THINNEST bucket (+6.90) where the club slices cannot vote at all; the staleness explanation for that was tested and rejected, since windowing the slices is worse at every length (cumulative +2.17 against last-8 +1.85 and last-60 +1.96). CORRECTED the same day on two counts. First, that ablation table was NOT selectivity-matched — the claim row keeps 25.7% of cards and the six-search row 41.8%, and a stricter rule buys hit rate for free, so the pairing did not support the weight put on it; the six-search line is a real result on its own terms. Second, matched properly, the score IS additive: blending claim rank with score rank optimises at w=0.2-0.5 rather than 0, and inside the claim's own top 40% the score sorts the survivors monotonically across quartiles in both windows. The six searches also double-count — in a league that leans one way "Pisa" and "Pisa on unders" are the same thirty cards — and making the crossing report a residual against the club's own rate instead of the league removes the overlap, holding the signal while cutting its cross-window gap from 1.38 points to 0.09. Then the bank was DOUBLED to 62,528 graded cards (1,500 fixtures per league, ~4 seasons) to settle the bettor's tank question directly, and the effect roughly HALVED: the quartile spread inside the claim's top 40% falls from +3.57 to +2.08 and the blend's gain from +0.10 to +0.07 at keep-40%. Everything survives in sign, nothing in size — more data shrank the finding rather than confirming it, which is the honest answer to whether filling the tank helps. Priced on 8,121 final picks at real closing odds: everything -1.67%, claim top 40% -1.57% at 86.2% hit, blended top 15% -0.96% (n=245, +/-2.03, a lead not a result), and claim-top-40-best-quartile -2.44% — WORSE than the claim alone in both windows at the same hit rate, because the extra selectivity picks shorter prices than it picks winners. Selection 1 is the whole project in one line: 4.4 points of hit rate bought 0.1 points of ROI while the average quote fell 1.16 to 1.13. RE-CHECKED once more and two claims fell. The red-tier INVERSION does not exist: it came from ~420 and ~1,630 cards on the shallow bank with the sign score, and on 62,528 cards with the de-duplicated continuous score red sorts the normal way at +2.29, positive in both windows. Promotion is still wrong, but for a different reason — red's BEST score quartile grades 79.27% against orange's WORST at 81.64%, so red's ceiling sits under orange's floor, which is a claim about levels the data supports rather than about direction, which it does not. And the era instability (+2.14 older / +0.90 newer shallow, against +1.48 / +3.04 deep) was the wrong cut entirely: it is not league mix, whose shares barely move between halves, it is REGION. The effect is European — +3.57 quartile spread in Europe with both windows positive, EXACTLY ZERO in the Americas at -0.06 (and -0.41 in one half), and RoW at +4.27 on 5,929 cards swinging +5.77 to +1.75, too thin to read. So the finding is not 'a card can be scored against its own searches' but 'a card in a European league can be'. That also kills the blended top-15% selection as a lead: it is 23.8% RoW against 9.5% of the bank, two and a half times over-represented in exactly the region where the score does not work, so its -0.96% on 245 cards is a noisy sub-population landing favourably rather than a signal. Club memory swept properly — windowing ONLY the club reads, leaving league and side cumulative — is NEUTRAL, every setting from last-20 to last-730-days within 0.15 points; last-40 ships as a reasoned default because it never loses and squads really do change, not as a measured gain. That sweep also explains an earlier wrong conclusion: the previous windowing test cut the league-side slice too, a statistic built from hundreds of cards with no staleness problem, which is why it showed harm. Orange because the residual is worth a fourth, small, ONE-DIRECTIONAL input to the existing guard, applied to EUROPEAN leagues only, sized for +2 to +3.5 points — and not a five-colour system, not a reroute rule, and not a reason to enlarge the bank |

By tier: green 87.0% hit / -1.05%, orange 82.5% / -1.08%, red 75.8% / -4.30%. Dropping red lifts the book to -1.07%, so the answer to 'will red take out most of the bad ones' is yes on strike rate. But the two-window split refuses the money claim: older half red -1.17% against green -0.91% and orange -0.88% — worth nothing — while the newer half runs red -6.28%. The hitrate gap holds in BOTH halves (86.7 vs 77.2 older, 87.3 vs 74.9 newer), so what red really marks is a lower strike rate that the market sometimes prices correctly and sometimes does not. Independent support from the thin-league run the same day: green -2.47% against orange -4.40% on 836 picks. Ships as a CONFIDENCE colour on the card, not as a profitability filter — -1.07% is still a loss, and half the evidence for the lift is one time period |
| 🟠 | 09-01 | engine | **Are the goal-expectation ratings better built from SHOTS ON TARGET than from goals? (the store already holds hst/ast; goals are the noisy realisation and shots regress better between matches)** | Measured and promising, not yet in the engine. scripts/shot_ratings.py replays both models as-of in one forward pass with an identical functional form, window, shrinkage and Poisson scoring — only the statistic differs — and scores them on U1.5/U2.5/U3.5/U4.5, four lines neither model selects. Run over ALL 128,532 fixtures the store holds. Shots ALONE are not reliable — -1.09% overall, and -2.37% in the older half, which is what patchy shot recording in the thinner leagues and older seasons looks like. But a MIXTURE beats goals alone and does it in both windows independently: w=0.4 on shots gives +0.96% of Brier overall, +0.80% in the older half (which prefers w=0.3) and +1.24% in the newer (which prefers w=0.5). The optimum is a flat basin from 0.3 to 0.5, not a spike, which is what a real effect looks like rather than a fitted one. On the 8 richest leagues alone the same sweep gives +1.07% at w=0.6. For scale, the whole gap to the bookmaker's closing line is 2.3%, so this is roughly half of it. CAVEAT that governs what happens next: the baseline here is a standalone goals model scoring 0.1925, while the real engine scores 0.1876 — it is already better than the thing shots beat, so the gain cannot be assumed to transfer. The next step is the shots signal inside the real pipeline, re-measured the same way. One bug found and fixed mid-experiment: an all-time cumulative conversion rate penalised only the shots model and reversed the whole result, turning +0.35% into -3.01% |
| 🟠 | 09-01 | board | **Star tip 3 only when it is a DNB claiming more than 2 points above tip 1 — otherwise always star tip 1 (fell out of testing the profile chooser above)** | Validated and not yet shipped. It drops to tip 3 whenever tip 1 is not playable, and tip 3's baseline sits five points below tip 1's, so it trades down by construction. The gate fixes the trade: on the 426 cards where a DNB out-claims tip 1 by more than 2 points, the DNB grades 94.13% against tip 1's 82.16%, +11.97. It holds with DNB pushes stripped out entirely (+9.58 on 355 cards), so it is not a settlement artefact, and it clears BOTH windows independently (+7.44 older, +16.59 newer; +5.79 and +13.94 with pushes removed). It also holds whether or not tip 1 is playable (+11.38 playable, +13.86 not), so it needs no playable exception. DOUBLE CHANCE must be excluded: at the same gate a DC switch LOSES 3.53 points. Whole-board effect is small because it fires on 1.42% of cards (+0.170 points over always-tip-1) — the real gain is the 1.7 points from retiring the current rule |

The feared Poisson draw bias never appears — TEAM_SHRINK compresses the sides and pumps the draw by roughly what Poisson drops it. The strongest-DC band that supplies three quarters of prints grades +0.1/−0.5; every stronger band and every DNB band UNDERCLAIMS (+1.3 to +8.5) — the lane's failure mode is modesty. Prints from DC_FLOOR 0.72 with edge ≥2 over the league's as-of base rate; DNB upgrade at 0.65 of the no-draw mass. Outside every tally and the hero until live results earn it in, exactly the cup lane's probation path |
| 🟠 | 08-29 | engine | **A push should count as a win at the ROOT, not just in the tallies — the bettor's rule: the standing offset plays U3.0 as U3.5, so the actual play wins where the printed rung pushes** | The board's counters, playable tallies and hero window now grade ◦ as a hit (shipped 29 Aug). The deeper change — hit_weight("push")=1.0, which would fold push totals into winning_totals and hence into the PUBLISHED probabilities and selection ties — is a real engine change: U3.0 would collapse into the U3.25/U3.5 tier, tips would reshuffle, and every baseline (retrosim table, REL bases, floors) needs re-derivation on two windows before it ships. Until then the claims exclude the push and the tally credits it, which overstates hit vs says by the push rate (~1-2 points on U3.0-heavy lanes) — known, accepted, and queued |
| 🟠 | 08-28 | engine | **The buy≥ gate prices REL-debited and capped lanes from the raw engine number** | _undebited() reverses the cup-over and high-says debits but not REL or the consensus cap, so a debited lane's printed buy≥ sits a few percent below its honest break-even. Capped lanes all land sub-bar where nothing is bought, and REL rarely bites hard on board lanes — small, but the gate run backwards should someday reverse everything the gate applies |
| 🟠 | 08-28 | ledger | **The widened staking strategy — taking lanes under buy≥ when probability still clears** | The bettor's own call, now instrumented: 18 positions logged on the Session #4 slate, 8 on engine-priced rungs of which 1 sits above buy≥, 3 between break-even and buy≥, and 4 marginally under stated break-even (−0.01 to −0.04). The played-lane record historically grades 1–2 points ABOVE its stated probability, which covers most of that shortfall — but not with a margin. The ledger measures it live: alignment column plus stored odds settle the question in a week of results. Systematized 28 Aug as the buy-blend (BUY_BLEND_BELOW/ABOVE): the printed buy≥ itself now reaches toward the league's playable record and prints the margin it gave up, so the widened strategy is priced on the card instead of eyeballed against it |
| 🟠 | 08-27 | data | **Official tables can diverge from results tables (points deductions)** | CSL 2026: Shenhua 28 pts official vs 35 by results, Taishan 33 vs 39, Guoan 36 vs 40 — every stored result verified correct against ESPN, so the gap is federation deductions the walked table cannot see. Reads now say 'by results'; no engine impact (mu never reads standings, and the pos_signed term was measured and declined). Open question: whether any consumer ever NEEDS the official table |
| 🟠 | 08-27 | cups | **The playoff DECIDER round may run hotter than the lane prices** | Live probation evidence from the 27 Aug slate: U4.25 tips stating 87–91 covered 20/28 (71%) across the run's cup fixtures — six 5+ goal matches in one night — while our O1.5 tips landed at their stated rate (calibrated, not underrated). One knockout round, ~2 SE, rhymes with the qualifiers' standing −3.2/−3.5 gaps. Untested: whether elimination-night second legs are structurally hotter than earlier rounds. Measure before the next playoff window |
| 🟠 | 08-27 | engine | **Balance means hit × buy≥, and five leagues fail it** | EV at the printed buy-from across the table runs 1.02–1.15. The extremes people worry about are HONEST — ITA-SB (77.2 hit, 1.46 odds) sits near the top at 1.127, JPN-J1 (88.6, 1.20) clears 1.063 — but the overconfident rows cannot cover their own price: MLS 1.017, ESP-L2 1.025, MEX-LMX 1.027, UEL-Q 1.033, FRA-L2 1.055. Their fix is per-league calibration or a cull, not floor tuning. ESP-L2 and MEX-LMX resolved 28 Aug by the consensus cap (no playable lanes to misprice); MLS, UEL-Q and FRA-L2 still pending |
| 🟠 | 08-27 | engine | **TUR-SL and ESP-L2 are overconfident in their RECENT two seasons** | The only two rows that survive the noise-aware re-measure: TUR-SL −5.2 (n 391), ESP-L2 −4.3 (n 525) — both read fine over a longer trail (−2.6 / −2.8 at n≈740), so the miss is concentrated in the current window. Cause not yet diagnosed |
| 🟠 | 08-27 | cups | **The whole Club Elo cup lane** | PROBATIONARY. 92% coverage, all windows same-signed, a 202-fixture dress rehearsal at 89.1% and a pooled gap of +0.1 after B3 — but cups have failed this project twice before, so it stays labelled until a full slate of live results confirms it |
| 🟠 | 08-26 | cups | **Level-aggregate second legs are decided at home, not through goals** | Profiled on 645 ties: goals ordinary (2.74 v 2.67 usual) but the home side wins 49.1% and 21.7% go to extra time. Real, and printed as context beside the tip — it is not yet a term in the mu |
| 🟠 | 08-26 | cups | **First-leg caution has faded** | Second legs run 2.81 goals against first legs' 2.59 and hold both windows, but the caution effect is gone (older −0.19, t −4.28; newer +0.02). Recorded, not acted on |
